# KSessions Vol 1 — Adam Cycle Teachings
*Source: Asif Hussain's delivered lectures on Asas al-Taweel, Group 18 (Asaas Al-Taveel)*
*Scope: Sequences 1-8 (Adam cycle; relevant to Vol 1: Pre-text + Adam)*
*Total: 42,994 words, 8 sessions*

These are live transcripts of delivered teachings directly on the content of Asas al-Taweel.
They contain: ta'wil explanations, esoteric readings, pedagogical framing, and contemporary
application of the prophetic cycles covered in Vol 1.

---

## Session 1: Preface

*Source: KSessions Group 18, Session ID 1276, 1504 words*

Preface & Introduction to the book

     

 Preface and Introduction

 With the support and grace of Allah, we have authored a book for those who answer the call of truth. It is for those who hold firmly to the rope of the Creator, cling to the firmest handhold, and adhere to the ideal path in the apparent knowledge of the divine law الشَّرِيعَةِ . We named this book "Pillars of Islam" دَعَائِمِ الْإِسْلَامِ , in which we outline the boundaries of faith, the distinction between faith الْإِيمَانِ and submission الْإِسْلَامِ , the obligation of allegiance الْوِلَايَةِ , guidance to the true leaders, the requirements of obligatory acts, and chapters on the lawful and unlawful, along with rulings and judgments.

 This foundational text enables believers to understand the outward aspects of their religion, internalize them, and act upon them without deviation or neglect. It represents the first stage of learning and the most basic level of comprehension. This is the starting point for anyone in whom the spirit of faith and wisdom has been instilled, and who has moved from the darkness of ignorance into the light of knowledge. This process is analogous to the initial care of a newborn. Just as a baby, emerging from the womb into the world, first has its external needs tended to—by cutting the umbilical cord, aligning its limbs, and wrapping its body for strength and support to prevent harm or deformity—so too must a believer’s outward practice of faith be established and fortified. Anyone who neglects to rectify and strengthen the apparent الظَّاهِرُ aspects of their religion risks its eventual collapse.

 We presented this guidance with the hope of fostering correction and integrity. We shared it, permitted its transcription, and made it widely accessible. A few benefited from it, as Allah says 

 Q|38:24

 Fusilat (41:4) 

 ‏ ‏بَشيرًۭا وَنَذيرًۭا فَأَعْرَضَ أَكْثَرُهُمْ فَهُمْ لَا يَسْمَعُونَ ‎  ‎

 Translation:.

 [As a giver of good tidings and a warner, but most of them turn away, so they do not hear. [4] ].

 The Romans (30:6). 

 ‏ ‏وَعْدَ ٱللَّه ۖ لَا يُخْلفُ ٱللَّهُ وَعْدَهُ وَلَكنَّ أَكْثَرَ ٱلنَّاس لَا يَعْلَمُونَ ‎  ‎

 Translation:.

 [[It is] the promise of Allah. Allah does not fail in His promise, but most people are unaware of this. [6] ].

 Joseph (12:103) 

 ‏ ‏وَمَا أَكْثَرُ ٱلنَّاس وَلَوْ حَرَصْتَ بمُؤْمنينَ ‎  ‎

 Translation:.

 [And most of the people, although you strive [for it], are not believers. [103] ].

 Q|3:110

 The ignorant and astray among them said, "This is merely the apparent knowledge we already have, and we see no benefit in it." Their statement mirrors those who rejected Allah’s messengers, about whom He says:

 The Criterion (25:4-5). 

 ‏ ‏وَقَالَ ٱلَّذينَ كَفَرُوا۟ إنْ هَذَا إلَّآ إفْكٌ ٱفْتَرَىٰهُ وَأَعَانَهُ عَلَيْه قَوْمٌ آخَرُونَ ۖ فَقَدْ جَاءُو ظُلْمًۭا وَزُورًۭا ‎ ‏وَقَالُوا۟ أَسَطيرُ ٱلْأَوَّلينَ ٱكْتَتَبَهَا فَهىَ تُمْلَىٰ عَلَيْه بُكْرَةًۭ وَأَصيلًۭا ‎ ‎

 Translation:.

 And those who disbelieve say, "This [Qur'an] is not except a falsehood he invented, and another people assisted him in it." But they have committed an injustice and a lie. And they say, "Legends of the former peoples which he has written down, and they are dictated to him morning and afternoon.".

 This is the path of those who deny the limits of religion, which Allah’s wisdom has ordained for all generations. This corruption was spread by evil preachers who were entrusted with responsibility by the guardians of Allah but betrayed that trust. They disobeyed commands, altered the established order, transgressed their limits, and prevented others from fulfilling their 

 The Table Spread (5:77). 

 ‏ ‏قُلْ يَأَهْلَ ٱلْكتَب لَا تَغْلُوا۟ فى دينكُمْ غَيْرَ ٱلْحَقّ وَلَا تَتَّبعُوا۟ أَهْوَاءَ قَوْمٍۢ قَدْ ضَلُّوا۟ من قَبْلُ وَأَضَلُّوا۟ كَثيرًۭا وَضَلُّوا۟ عَن سَوَاء ٱلسَّبيل ‎ ‎

 Translation:.

 Say, "o people of the Scripture, do not exceed limits in your religion beyond the truth and do not follow the inclinations of a people who had gone astray before and misled many and have strayed from the soundness of the way.".

 Had they followed the path defined by their guardians, affairs would have been set right, and there would be no grounds for dispute. Allah rectifies, through His guardian who dedicates himself to the cause, what has been altered in His religion, by His might, power, generosity, and mercy.

    

 A Gradual Path to Deeper Knowledge

    

 For the few who did preserve the knowledge of the apparent الظَّاهِرُ and reached their potential within it, it was incumbent upon us not to hold them back. We decided to elevate them to the next level, which pertains to the esoteric الْبَاطِنِ . This is the level of symbols and metaphors, the most subtle and accessible of the deeper limits of knowledge. This stage of spiritual development is akin to giving milk to an infant after its physical needs are met. Young children cannot handle solid food due to their delicate nature, so they are nourished with milk until their bodies are strong enough to digest more substantial nourishment gradually.

 Similarly, symbols and metaphors stimulate the imagination and senses of the listeners, preparing them for the wisdom to which these signs allude. This creates a yearning to hear more and a readiness to accept it. If this profound knowledge were presented all at once, without preparation, it would be harmful. It would be like forcing solid food upon an infant before the proper time. Allah has set a measure for this, saying:

 The Cow (2:233). 

 ‏ ‏ وَٱلْوَلدَتُ يُرْضعْنَ أَوْلَدَهُنَّ حَوْلَيْن كَاملَيْن ۖ لمَنْ أَرَادَ أَن يُتمَّ ٱلرَّضَاعَةَ وَعَلَى ٱلْمَوْلُود لَهُ رزْقُهُنَّ وَكسْوَتُهُنَّ بٱلْمَعْرُوف لَا تُكَلَّفُ نَفْسٌ إلَّا وُسْعَهَا لَا تُضَارَّ وَلدَةٌۢ بوَلَدهَا وَلَا مَوْلُودٌۭ لَّهُ بوَلَده وَعَلَى ٱلْوَارث مثْلُ ذَلكَ فَإنْ أَرَادَا فصَالًا عَن تَرَاضٍۢ مّنْهُمَا وَتَشَاوُرٍۢ فَلَا جُنَاحَ عَلَيْهمَا وَإنْ أَرَدتُّمْ أَن تَسْتَرْضعُوا۟ أَوْلَدَكُمْ فَلَا جُنَاحَ عَلَيْكُمْ إذَا سَلَّمْتُم مَّا آتَيْتُم بٱلْمَعْرُوف وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَٱعْلَمُوا۟ أَنَّ ٱللَّهَ بمَا تَعْمَلُونَ بَصيرٌۭ ‎ ‎

 Translation:.

 Mothers may breastfeed their children for two complete years, for whoever wishes to complete the nursing period. Upon the father is the mother's provision and their clothing, according to what is acceptable. No person is charged with more than his capacity. No mother should be harmed through her child, and no father through his child. And upon the [father's] heir is [a duty] like that [of the father]. And if they both desire weaning through mutual consent from both of them and consultation, there is no blame upon either of them. And if you wish to have your children nursed by a substitute, there is no blame upon you as long as you give payment according to what is acceptable. And fear Allah and know that Allah is seeing what you do.

 For this reason, after the "Pillars of Islam," we presented a book named "Limits of Knowledge" حُدُودَ الْمَعْرِفَةِ . In it, we established the proofs for allegorical interpretation التَّأْوِيلِ against those who deny it and for the inner meaning الْبَاطِنِ against those who reject it. We explained how to ascend through its levels, providing symbols and indications of the inner meanings to benefit those with an intellectual understanding. The people’s reaction to this book was the same as before, marked by acceptance and desire from a few, and aversion and detachment from the many.

    

 The Foundation of Interpretation

    

 Having reached this stage, we felt an obligation to those who were eager to learn more. We decided to present a book on a level of interpretation, التَّأْوِيلِ , and inner meaning, الْبَاطِنِ , that succeeds the level of symbolism. Anyone who hears this knowledge presented outside of its proper context or without the necessary preparation risks following the path of the rejectors, shedding the garment of their faith. 

 The Family of Imran (3:141). 

 ‏ ‏وَليُمَحّصَ ٱللَّهُ ٱلَّذينَ آمَنُوا۟ وَيَمْحَقَ ٱلْكَفرينَ ‎ ‎

 Translation:.

 And that Allah may purify the believers [through trials] and destroy the disbelievers.  

 If only they understood the words of the truthful and trustworthy one, Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq (AS). It was related that when he explained an esoteric matter to some of his close followers, one of them said, "O son of Rasul Allah (SWS), we have heard you say something different from this before." The Imam (AS) replied, «إِنَّا نَتَكَلَّمُ فِي الْكَلِمَةِ الْوَاحِدَةِ سَبْعَةَ أَوْجُهٍ» (Indeed, we speak of a single word in seven aspects). The man asked thoughtfully, "Seven, O son of Rasul Allah (SWS)?" The Imam (AS) said, «نَعَمْ وَسَبْعِينَ، وَلَوِ اسْتَزَادَنَا لَزِدْنَاهُ» (Yes, and seventy. And if they asked us for more, we would give more). The facets of this knowledge correspond to its various levels, and this is understood by whoever listens, benefits, and ascends through its ranks.

 This book, which we have now presented, outlines its fundamental principles. It is the first of its levels, and we have named it "Foundation of Interpretation" أَسَاسِ التَّأْوِيلِ . We aim to explain the concepts established in "Pillars of Islam," so that this work may serve as a foundation for the esoteric الْبَاطِنِ , just as the former serves as a foundation for the apparent الظَّاهِرِ . And in Allah we seek assistance; there is no power or strength except in Allah, the Great.

## Session 2: Validity Of Taveel

*Source: KSessions Group 18, Session ID 1277, 2119 words*

Validity Of Taveel

 

 كُنَّا بَسَطْنَا فِي كِتَابِ حُدُودِ الْمَعْرِفَةِ، الَّذِي قَدَّمْنَا ذِكْرَهُ، كَلَامًا طَوِيلًا فِي إِثْبَاتِ عِلْمِ التَّأْوِيلِ وَالْبَاطِنِ، وَالرَّدِّ عَلَى مَنْ أَنْكَرَهُ مِنَ الْكِتَابِ وَالسُّنَّةِ وَأَقْوَالِ الْأَئِمَّةِ وَالْأُمَّةِ، وَأَشْبَعْنَا الْقَوْلَ فِي ذَلِكَ، وَإِنْ كُنَّا قَدِ اخْتَصَرْنَاهُ.

 In the previously mentioned book "Limits of Knowledge," we provided an extensive discussion that demonstrates the science of interpretation and its profound significance. We also countered the arguments of those who reject it by referencing the Quran, the Sunnah, and the teachings of the Imams and the Muslim community. Although we summarized this topic, we covered it thoroughly.

 

 وَلَا يَنْبَغِي لَنَا أَنْ نُخْلِيَ صَدْرَ هَذَا الْكِتَابِ مِنْ طَرَفٍ مِنْهُ لِيَكُونَ حُجَّةً لَنَا بَيْنَ أَيْدِي مَنْ يَتْلُوهُ.

 And it is not appropriate for us to begin this book without a portion of it, so that it may serve as proof for those who read it.

 

 فَنَقُولُ وَبِاللَّهِ التَّوْفِيقُ:

 So we say with Allah (SWT)'s help:

 Q|2:34

 

 إِنَّهُ لَا بُدَّ لِكُلِّ مَحْسُوسٍ مِنْ ظَاهِرٍ وَبَاطِنٍ؛

 Indeed, every tangible thing must have an outer and an inner aspect;

 

 فَظَاهِرُهُ مَا تَقَعُ الْحَوَاسُّ عَلَيْهِ،

 Its outer aspect is what the senses perceive,

 

 وَبَاطِنُهُ مَا يَحْوِيهِ وَيُحِيطُ الْعِلْمُ بِهِ بِأَنَّهُ فِيهِ،

 and its inner aspect includes both its contents and the related knowledge contained within.

 

 وَظَاهِرُهُ مُشْتَمِلٌ عَلَيْهِ وَهُوَ زَوْجُهُ وَقَرِينُهُ.

 The outer aspect includes the inner, serving as its partner and counterpart.

 

 قَالَ اللَّهُ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ مِنْ قَائِلٍ: ﴿ وَمِنْ كُلِّ شَيْءٍ خَلَقْنَا زَوْجَيْنِ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَذَكَّرُونَ ﴾.

 Allah (SWT) said: "And We created pairs of all things so that you may remember."

 

 وَإِنَّ كُلَّمَا جَاءَ فِي الْحَدِيثِ وَالتَّنْزِيلِ شَيْءٌ، وَكُلَّ شَيْءٍ وَإِنْ كَانَ وَاحِدًا فَلَا بُدَّ لَهُ مِنْ زَوْجٍ إِبَانَةً لِوَحْدَةِ الْبَارِي الْبَائِنِ عَنْ خَلْقِهِ، وَلَا يَقُومُ شَيْءٌ مِنْ دُونِهِ إِلَّا بِمُزَاوَجَةٍ؛

 Indeed, whenever something is mentioned in the Hadith and Revelation, every entity, no matter how unique, needs a counterpart to highlight the oneness of the Creator, who exists independently of His creation. Nothing exists outside of Him without this pairing.

 

 كَالْإِنْسَانِ وَهُوَ شَخْصٌ وَاحِدٌ، إِلَّا أَنَّهُ جَسَدٌ وَرُوحٌ، فَالْجَسَدُ هُوَ الظَّاهِرُ وَالرُّوحُ هِيَ الْبَاطِنُ.

 Like a human being, he is a single person; however, he consists of both a body and a spirit. The body represents the outer self, while the spirit represents the inner self.

 

 وَكُلُّ وَاحِدٍ مِنَ الِاثْنَيْنِ مُرَكَّبٌ مِنْ شَيْئَيْنِ:

 And each one of the two is composed of two things:

 

 فَالْجَسَدُ مُرَكَّبٌ مِنَ الْبُرُودَةِ وَالْيُبُوسَةِ، وَالرُّوحُ مُرَكَّبَةٌ مِنَ الْحَرَارَةِ وَالرُّطُوبَةِ.

 So the body is composed of coldness and dryness, and the spirit is composed of heat and moisture.

 

 فَإِذَا فَارَقَتِ الرُّوحُ الْجَسَدَ بَقِيَ الْجَسَدُ بَارِدًا يَابِسًا.

 When the spirit leaves the body, it becomes cold and dry.

 

 وَلِذَلِكَ كُلُّ مَا فِي الْعَالَمِ إِذَا اعْتُبِرَ لَا بُدَّ لَهُ مِنَ الِازْدِوَاجِ.

 And for that reason, everything in the world, when considered, must have pairing.

 

 وَقَدْ ذَكَرَ اللَّهُ سُبْحَانَهُ الْبَاطِنَ فِي مَوَاضِعَ كَثِيرَةٍ مِنْ كِتَابِهِ

 And Allah (SWT), the Exalted, has mentioned the inner aspect in many places in His Book.

 

 فَقَالَ جَلَّ ثَنَاؤُهُ: ﴿ وَأَسْبَغَ عَلَيْكُمْ نِعَمَهُ ظَاهِرَةً وَبَاطِنَةً ﴾.

 And He, exalted is His praise, said: "And He has completed His favors upon you, apparent and hidden."

 

 وَأَخْبَرَ أَنَّهُ يَسْأَلُ عِبَادَهُ عَنْ نِعَمِهِ عَلَيْهِمْ

 And He informed that He will ask His servants about His favors upon them

 

 فَقَالَ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ: ﴿ ثُمَّ لَتُسْأَلُنَّ يَوْمَئِذٍ عَنِ النَّعِيمِ ﴾.

 So He, the Mighty and Majestic, said: "Then you will surely be asked that Day about the blessings."

 

 فَمَنْ لَمْ يَعْرِفْ بَاطِنَ النَّعِيمِ مَا هُوَ؟

 So whoever does not know what the inner meaning of this "blessings" is?

 

 وَقَدْ عَلِمَ أَنَّهُ مَسْؤُولٌ عَنْهُ، وَكَيْفَ يَكُونُ جَوَابُهُ إِذَا سُئِلَ عَنْهُ؟

 He knows he will be asked about it; how will he respond when the question comes up?

 

 ثُمَّ قَالَ سُبْحَانَهُ لَا شَرِيكَ لَهُ: ﴿ وَذَرُوا ظَاهِرَ الْإِثْمِ وَبَاطِنَهُ ﴾.

 Then He, the Exalted, who has no partner, said: "And leave the outer and inner forms of sin."

 

 فَمَنْ لَمْ يَعْرِفْ بَاطِنَ الْإِثْمِ فَيَذَرَهُ، أَلَيْسَ يُخْشَى عَلَيْهِ مِنْ أَنْ يَقَعَ فِيهِ إِذَا جَهِلَهُ؟

 Therefore, if someone does not grasp the true nature of sin, should they not be concerned that their lack of understanding will lead them into it?

 

 ثُمَّ ذَكَرَ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ فِي كِتَابِهِ فَقَالَ: ﴿ هُوَ الَّذِي أَنْزَلَ عَلَيْكَ الْكِتَابَ مِنْهُ آيَاتٌ مُحْكَمَاتٌ هُنَّ أُمُّ الْكِتَابِ وَأُخَرُ مُتَشَابِهَاتٌ ﴾ وَجْهَهُ.

 Then He, the Mighty and Majestic, referenced in His Book, declared: "It is He who has sent down to you the Book; it contains clear verses, which form the foundation of the Book, along with others that are allegorical." This captures its essence.

 

 وَقَدْ ذَكَرْنَا فِي حُدُودِ الْمَعْرِفَةِ قَوْلَ مَنْ أَقَرَّ بِالتَّأْوِيلِ وَجَهِلَ، وَقَوْلَ مَنْ دَفَعَهُ وَأَنْكَرَهُ، وَالْحُجَّةَ عَلَى جَمِيعِهِمْ.

 And we have mentioned in [the book] "Limits of Knowledge" the saying of whoever acknowledged interpretation but was ignorant [about it], and the saying of whoever rejected and denied it, and the proof against all of them.

 

 وَقَالَ سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى فِي التَّأْوِيلِ إِلَى قَوْلِهِ: ﴿ وَمَا يَعْلَمُ تَأْوِيلَهُ إِلَّا اللَّهُ وَالرَّاسِخُونَ فِي الْعِلْمِ ﴾،

 And He, the Exalted and Most High, said concerning interpretation, up to His saying: "And no one knows its interpretation except Allah (SWT) and those firm in knowledge,"

 

 ﴿ هَلْ يَنْظُرُونَ إِلَّا تَأْوِيلَهُ يَوْمَ يَأْتِي تَأْوِيلُهُ يَقُولُ الَّذِينَ نَسُوهُ مِنْ قَبْلُ قَدْ جَاءَتْ رُسُلُ رَبِّنَا بِالْحَقِّ ﴾.

 "Do they await anything but its fulfillment? On the Day its fulfillment comes, those who disregarded it before will say, 'Our Lord's messengers had indeed come with the truth.'"

 

 وَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ وَسَلَّمَ: « مَا نَزَلَتْ عَلَيَّ مِنَ الْقُرْآنِ آيَةٌ إِلَّا وَلَهَا ظَهْرٌ وَبَطْنٌ ».

 And the Messenger of Allah PBUH said: "No verse descended upon me from the Quran except that it has an outer and an inner meaning."

 

 وَقَدْ ذَكَرْنَا فِي كِتَابِ حُدُودِ الْمَعْرِفَةِ إِجْمَاعَ الْأُمَّةِ عَلَى صِحَّةِ هَذَا الْخَبَرِ، وَفَسَادَ تَأْوِيلِ مَنْ تَأَوَّلَهُ عَلَى غَيْرِ الصَّوَابِ فِيهِ.

 In [the book] "Limits of Knowledge," we highlighted the community's agreement on the authenticity of this report and the inaccuracies in the interpretations made by those who misunderstood it.

 

 وَذَكَرْنَا مَا أَقَرُّوا بِهِ مِنَ الْبَاطِنِ لَمَّا لَمْ يَجِدُوا مَحِيصًا دُونَ الْإِقْرَارِ بِهِ، وَلَزِمَهُمُ ذَلِكَ الضَّرُورَةُ فِيهِ، إِذْ لَمْ يَجِدُوا مَخْرَجًا مِنْهُ، نَظِيرَ الْمَاءِ.

 They recognized the inner meaning only when escape proved impossible. Necessity forced this acknowledgment, much like water finding its way out.

 

 وَقَوْلُهُمْ إِنَّ اللَّهَ سُبْحَانَهُ أَرَادَ بِهِ الْعِلْمَ فِي مَوْضِعٍ، وَالْمَوْتَ وَالْكُفْرَ فِي مَوْضِعٍ آخَرَ، وَبِالْحَيَاةِ الْإِيمَانَ، وَبِالنُّورِ الْهُدَى، وَبِالظَّلَامِ الضَّلَالَةَ، وَبِالْعَمَى وَالصَّمَمِ وَالْبَكَمِ غَيْرَ مَا هُوَ فِي الظَّاهِرِ، وَبِالنِّعَاجِ النِّسَاءَ، وَكَثِيرٍ مِنْ مِثْلِ ذَلِكَ مِمَّا يَطُولُ شَرْحُهُ.

 They suggest that Allah (SWT), the Exalted, intended knowledge in one context, and death and disbelief in another. By "life," He refers to faith; by "light," He signifies guidance; and by "darkness," He alludes to misguidance. Additionally, the terms "blindness," "deafness," and "muteness" carry meanings beyond their obvious definitions, and "ewes" (female sheep) symbolizes women, among many other interpretations that could be elaborated on extensively.

 

 فَإِذَا سَمِعُوا مِنْ مِثْلِ ذَلِكَ مَا يُوجِبُ تَوْحِيدَ اللَّهِ تَعَالَى وَتَنْزِيهَهُ عَنْ صِفَاتِ خَلْقِهِ أَنْكَرُوهُ،

 When they heard about matters that necessitate affirming the oneness of Allah (SWT), the Most High, and declaring Him free from the attributes of His creation, they denied it.

 

 وَشَبَّهُوا مَعْبُودَهُمْ، وَوَصَفُوهُ وَنَسَبُوهُ إِلَى مَا يَتَعَالَى اللَّهُ جَلَّ ثَنَاؤُهُ، وَيَتَقَدَّسُ وَيَتَبَارَكُ اسْمُهُ عَنْهُ.

 They compared their object of worship to Him, described Him, and ascribed to Him qualities that Allah (SWT), exalted is His praise, is far above. Holy is He, and blessed is His name beyond such notions.

 

 وَقَدْ سَمِعُوا مَا ذَكَرَ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ فِي كِتَابِهِ مِنْ ضَرْبِ الْأَمْثَالِ لِعِبَادِهِ كَقَوْلِهِ تَعَالَى:

 They have already been informed about what He, the Mighty and Majestic, stated in His Book regarding the parables He illustrates for His servants, such as His saying, the Most High:

 

 ﴿ وَتِلْكَ الْأَمْثَالُ نَضْرِبُهَا لِلنَّاسِ وَمَا يَعْقِلُهَا إِلَّا الْعَالِمُونَ ﴾.

 "And these parables We present to the people, and none will understand them except those of knowledge."

 

 وَقَوْلِهِ: ﴿ إِنَّ اللَّهَ لَا يَسْتَحْيِي أَنْ يَضْرِبَ مَثَلًا مَا بَعُوضَةً فَمَا فَوْقَهَا فَأَمَّا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا فَيَعْلَمُونَ أَنَّهُ الْحَقُّ مِنْ رَبِّهِمْ وَأَمَّا الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا فَيَقُولُونَ مَاذَا أَرَادَ اللَّهُ بِهَذَا مَثَلًا يُضِلُّ بِهِ كَثِيرًا وَيَهْدِي بِهِ كَثِيرًا وَمَا يُضِلُّ بِهِ إِلَّا الْفَاسِقِينَ ﴾.

 He said: "Certainly, Allah (SWT) is not hesitant to give an example – that of a mosquito or even something larger. Those who believe recognize it as the truth from their Lord. However, those who disbelieve question, 'What does Allah (SWT) mean by this example?' He leads many astray with it and guides many as well. But He does not mislead except the exceedingly disobedient."

 

 وَقَوْلِهِ: ﴿ وَكُلًّا ضَرَبْنَا لَهُ الْأَمْثَالَ وَكُلًّا تَبَّرْنَا تَتْبِيرًا ﴾.

 And His saying: "And for all, We presented examples, and all We destroyed with [utter] destruction."

 

 وَقَوْلِهِ: ﴿ وَلَقَدْ ضَرَبْنَا لِلنَّاسِ فِي هَذَا الْقُرْآنِ مِنْ كُلِّ مَثَلٍ ﴾.

 And His saying: "And We have certainly presented for the people in this Quran from every [kind of] example."

 

 وَأَخْبَرَ سُبْحَانَهُ أَنَّ لِلْأَحَادِيثِ أَيْضًا تَأْوِيلًا فَقَالَ فِي قِصَّةِ يُوسُفَ عَلَيْهِ السَّلَامُ:

 And He, the Exalted, informed that narratives also have interpretation, so He said concerning the story of Joseph, peace be upon him:

 

 ﴿ وَكَذَلِكَ يَجْتَبِيكَ رَبُّكَ وَيُعَلِّمُكَ مِنْ تَأْوِيلِ الْأَحَادِيثِ ﴾.

 "And thus will your Lord choose you and teach you the interpretation of narratives."

 

 وَقَالَ: ﴿ وَكَذَلِكَ مَكَّنَّا لِيُوسُفَ فِي الْأَرْضِ وَلِنُعَلِّمَهُ مِنْ تَأْوِيلِ الْأَحَادِيثِ ﴾.

 And He said: "And thus We established Joseph in the land that We might teach him the interpretation of narratives."

 

 وَفِي آيَاتٍ كَثِيرَةٍ مِنْ كِتَابِهِ سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى ذِكْرٌ لِلْأَمْثَالِ وَالْبَاطِنِ وَالتَّأْوِيلِ، وَذَلِكَ مَعْرُوفٌ فِي لِسَانِ الْعَرَبِ الَّذِي نَزَلَ الْقُرْآنُ بِهِ وَخَاطَبَهُمْ بِلِسَانِهِمْ فِيهِ.

 And in many verses of His Book, He, the Exalted and Most High, mentions parables, the inner meaning, and interpretation, and that is well-known in the language of the Arabs with which the Quran was revealed and with which He addressed them.

 

 وَذَلِكَ مِنْ مُعْجِزَاتِ وَغَرَائِبِ تَأْلِيفِهِ؛ أَنَّهُ يَأْتِي بِالشَّيْءِ الْوَاحِدِ وَلَهُ مَعْنًى فِي ظَاهِرِهِ وَمَعْنًى فِي بَاطِنِهِ.

 And that is among the miracles and wonders of its composition; that it presents one thing that has a meaning in its outer aspect and another meaning in its inner aspect.

 

 فَجَعَلَ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ ظَاهِرَهُ مُعْجِزَةَ رَسُولِهِ، وَبَاطِنَهُ مُعْجِزَةَ الْأَئِمَّةِ مِنْ أَهْلِ بَيْتِهِ، لَا يُوجَدُ إِلَّا عِنْدَهُمْ.

 Thus, He, the Mighty and Majestic, transformed its external appearance into the miracle of His Messenger, while its internal reality represents the miracle of the Imams from His household, a phenomenon unique to them.

 

 وَلَا يَسْتَطِيعُ أَحَدٌ أَنْ يَأْتِيَ بِظَاهِرِ الْكِتَابِ غَيْرَ مُحَمَّدٍ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ وَسَلَّمَ جَدِّهِمْ،

 And no one is able to bring the outer meaning of the Book other than Muhammad, the Messenger of Allah PBUH, their ancestor,

 

 وَلَا أَنْ يَأْتِيَ بِبَاطِنِهِ غَيْرَ الْأَئِمَّةِ مِنْ ذُرِّيَّتِهِ.

 and no one is able to bring its inner meaning other than the Imams from his descendants.

 

 وَهُوَ عِلْمٌ مُتَوَافِرٌ بَيْنَهُمْ مُسْتَوْدَعٌ فِيهِمْ؛

 And they possess extensive knowledge embedded within them;

 

 يُخَاطِبُونَ كُلَّ قَوْمٍ مِنْهُ بِمِقْدَارِ مَا يَفْهَمُونَ،

 They engage everyone based on their level of understanding,

 

 وَيُعْطُونَ كُلَّ أَهْلِ حَدٍّ مِنْهُ مَا يَسْتَحِقُّونَ،

 and they allocate to each individual of a specific rank what they merit,

 

 وَيَمْنَعُونَ مِنْهُ مَنْ يَجِبُ مَنْعُهُ،

 and they keep it from those who should not have it,

 

 وَيَدْفَعُونَ عَنْهُ مَنِ اسْتَحَقَّ دَفْعَهُ،

 and they ward off from it whoever deserves to be warded off,

 

 لِقَوْلِ الْعَزِيزِ الْوَهَّابِ: ﴿ هَذَا عَطَاؤُنَا فَامْنُنْ أَوْ أَمْسِكْ بِغَيْرِ حِسَابٍ ﴾.

 because of the saying of the All-Mighty, the Bestower: "This is Our bestowal; so give or withhold without reckoning."

 

 وَنَحْنُ الْآنَ بِعَوْنِ اللَّهِ وَتَأْيِيدِهِ نَبْتَدِئُ بِذِكْرِ مَا شَرَطْنَا ذِكْرَهُ مِنْ تَأْوِيلِ مَا بَسَطْنَاهُ فِي كِتَابِ دَعَائِمِ الْإِسْلَامِ،

 With Allah's (SWT) guidance, we will now explore the interpretation of our discussions in the book "Pillars of Islam."

 

 وَنَذْكُرُ بَاطِنَهُ عَلَى مَا يُوجِبُ هَذَا الْحَدُّ الَّذِي ابْتَدَأْنَا بِهِ،

 and we will discuss its deeper significance based on the approach we initially outlined,

 

 بِلِسَانِ مَنْ تُسْمِعُهُ إِيَّاهُ، وَمِقْدَارِ فَهْمِهِ.

 using the language of the audience we are addressing, tailored to their level of understanding.

 

 وَقَدْ قَالَ اللَّهُ سُبْحَانَهُ: ﴿ وَمَا أَرْسَلْنَا مِنْ رَسُولٍ إِلَّا بِلِسَانِ قَوْمِهِ لِيُبَيِّنَ لَهُمْ ﴾.

 And Allah (SWT), the Exalted, has said: "And We did not send any messenger except [speaking] the language of his people to make [the message] clear to them."

 

 لِأَنَّ الْعَامَّةَ إِذَا خُوطِبُوا بِلِسَانِ الْخَاصَّةِ، لَمَا فَهِمُوا أَكْثَرَ الْخِطَابِ.

 If ordinary people are spoken to using elitist language, much of the conversation will be lost on them.

 

 وَقَصَدْنَا بِالْمُخَاطَبَةِ مَا يَقْرُبُ مِنْ أَفْهَامِ السَّامِعِينَ، وَلَمْ نُخْرِجْهَا عَلَى مَخَارِجِ أَلْفَاظِ الْمَنْطِقِيِّينَ وَالْفَلْسَفِيِّينَ، كَمَا فَعَلَ ذَلِكَ بَعْضُ الْمُتَكَلِّمِينَ.

 We aimed our address at what resonates with the listeners' understanding, rather than using the terminology of logicians and philosophers, as some theologians have done.

 

 وَتَوَكَّلْنَا عَلَى اللَّهِ رَبِّنَا وَهُوَ حَسْبُ الْمُتَوَكِّلِينَ.

 And we rely upon Allah (SWT), our Lord, and He is sufficient for those who rely [on Him].

## Session 3: Faith and Submission in Islam

*Source: KSessions Group 18, Session ID 1278, 313 words*

The Foundations of the Prophets' Laws

 Each Messenger of strong will ( أُولِي الْعَزْمِ ) had two foundations for their divine law: a visible, external one and a hidden, internal one.

 The outward forms varied, but the inner essence remained the same: submission to Allah.

 Examples of Foundations:

 نوح (Noah): The Ark ( السَّفِينَةُ ) - a physical vessel for salvation.

 إبراهيم (Abraham): The Kaaba ( الْكَعْبَةُ ) - a focal point for pilgrimage and devotion.

 موسى (Moses): The Staff ( الْعَصَا ) - a symbol of divine power against falsehood.

 عيسى (Jesus): The Cross ( الصَّلِيبُ ) - representing sacrifice and commitment.

 محمد (Muhammad): The Testimony of Faith ( شَهَادَةُ أَنْ لَا إِلَهَ إِلَّا اللَّهُ ) - the declaration of Allah's Oneness.

 The Meaning of Sincere Testimony

 The Prophet Muhammad (PBUH) taught that the testimony ( الشهادة ) is the foundation of Islam and the key to Paradise.

 He clarified that sincerity ( الإخلاص ) in saying "La ilaha illa Allah" means "believing in it and knowing its limits" ( اعْتِقَادُهَا وَمَعْرِفَةُ حُدُودِهَا ).

 A sincere declaration of faith must be a combination of: Speech with the tongue ( قَوْلٌ بِاللِّسَانِ )

 Confirmation by the heart ( تَصْدِيقٌ بِالْجَنَانِ )

 Action with the limbs ( عَمَلٌ بِالْأَرْكَانِ )

 

 The testimony is not just a verbal statement; it is a profound covenant that encompasses all the requirements of the divine law and the obligations of worship.

 The Significance of the Testimony

 The testimony is the foundation of the شريعة (Shariah) and the core of the Messenger's call.

 It includes the Oneness of Allah ( توحيد الله ), affirmation of His Messenger, and the Imams of the religion.

 It necessitates obedience to their commands and acceptance of their authority ( ولاية ).

 The very letters ( الحروف ) of the testimony hold a deep, hidden wisdom, as names and entities are known through them.

## Session 4: The Fall Of Adam

*Source: KSessions Group 18, Session ID 1280, 9621 words*

Fall Of Adam

 Okay, let's delve into more detail regarding the Ismaili interpretation of Adam's Fall, drawing further from the provided text analyzed by Bernard Lewis.

 Deeper Dive into "An Ismaili Interpretation of the Fall of Adam"

 Building on the previous analysis, here are more specific details from Husain ibn 'Ali's Kitābu'l-Idah wa'l-Bayan , as presented in the text:

 1. Intensified Questioning of the Literal Narrative 

 The author's initial questioning goes beyond simple curiosity; it aims to dismantle the logical foundation of the literal story. Specific points raised include:

 The Tree's Nature: If the tree was good and desirable, why the prohibition? If bad, why did God, who knows all, create it in Paradise, especially knowing it would lead to Adam's expulsion and suffering?

 Specific Identifications: If, as some commentators suggested, the tree was wheat, why forbid it in Paradise but allow it freely on Earth to everyone, believers and non-believers alike?

 The 'Intestines' Argument: A peculiar traditional explanation suggested Adam lacked intestines, and fruit would rot inside him. The author dismisses this, asking why that specific tree was forbidden if any food would pose the same problem.

 Justice for Descendants: The author strongly questions why Adam's descendants were excluded from Paradise initially, emphasizing the injustice of punishing them for their ancestor's sin. He refutes the argument that God foreknew their unworthiness, pointing out God also foreknew Adam's transgression yet placed him in Paradise first. Why wasn't Adam treated like his descendants from the start?

 2. Nuances in the Historical/Religious Interpretation 

 The Tree as Knowledge/Rank: The text elaborates that the forbidden "tree" symbolizes the ʿilm ḥaqīqī (العلم الحقيقي), the true, esoteric knowledge. Accessing this knowledge leads to spiritual immortality (connecting to the Quranic "tree of immortality," shajarat al-khuld , شجرة الخلد ). There are differing views among the Ḥudūd (الحدود - ranks or teachers within the Ismaili hierarchy): some see the tree as the high rank of the Qāʾim (القائم), while others identify it with Adam's Waṣī (وصي - successor, Abel/هابيل), who possessed the taʾwīl (تأويل - esoteric interpretation) of Adam's sharīʿa (شريعة - religious law).

 The Murder of Abel: Iblis tricked Adam into revealing Abel's identity as the successor. Iblis then used this knowledge to incite jealousy in Cain (قابيل), telling him their father favored Abel. The text describes Cain killing Abel first "religiously" ( qatalahu qatlan dīnīyan , قتله قتلاً دينياً) and then "naturally" ( wabāʿda dhālika ṭabīʿīyan , وبعد ذلك طبيعياً). This suggests a spiritual attack or undermining of Abel's religious authority preceded the physical act.

 Ranks and Progression: Adam's fall involved a descent from the highest rank of Taʾyīd (التأييد - divine support/inspiration) to Taʿlīm (التعليم - teaching) and finally to the basic level of Mustajīb (المستجيب - respondent/initiate). The Mustajīb simply follows the external laws ( al-aʿmāl al-ẓāhira , الاعمال الظاهرة) without deep understanding, awaiting advancement. Repentance allowed Adam to return to the rank of Taʾyīd . His descendants ( dhurriya , ذرية), the converts ( al-mustajībūna li daʿwatihi , المستجيبون لدعوته), start at this lower level ("on earth"), adhering to the literal law before initiation into inner meanings.

 Kashf and Satr : Adam's unique position was because he came at the end of a cycle of Kashf (كشف - unveiling/openness) and initiated a new cycle of Satr (ستر - concealment). He already possessed the inner knowledge ( al-bāṭin al-maḥḍ , الباطن المحض) from the previous era and didn't need the gradual progression required for his followers in the era of concealment. This prior knowledge is why he was "in the garden" from the start.

 Ta'yid

 In Ismaili philosophy, تائید (Ta'yid) takes on a specialized and significant theological meaning that goes beyond its general linguistic sense of strengthening or support.

 In this context, تائید refers to divine inspiration or spiritual support that emanates from God and flows through the spiritual hierarchy. This hierarchy is central to Ismaili cosmology and includes the Universal Intellect, the Universal Soul, and the Imams.

 Key aspects of the meaning of تائید in Ismaili philosophy include:

 Divine Illumination: Ta'yid is the divine light or energy that illuminates the hearts and minds of those who are spiritually receptive, enabling them to grasp deeper, esoteric truths.

 Source of True Knowledge: It is considered the ultimate source of true, spiritual knowledge (haqa'iq), particularly the understanding of the inner, hidden (batin) dimensions of revelation and existence.

 Role of the Imam: The Imam, as the spiritual inheritor of the Prophet's light and knowledge, is the primary recipient and conduit of Ta'yid in each era. He is the one who possesses the fullness of this divine support.

 Spiritual Guidance: Ta'yid empowers the Imam and those of high spiritual rank within the Ismaili hierarchy (like the Hujjats and Da'is) to provide authoritative guidance (ta'lim) and esoteric interpretation (ta'wil) of religious texts and matters.

 Spiritual Connection: It signifies a direct spiritual connection between the devotee and the Imam, through which the devotee can receive spiritual blessings, strength, and insight.

 Beyond Ordinary Knowledge: Ta'yid is distinct from acquired or rational knowledge (`ilm muktasab). It is a gift of divine grace that grants a deeper, intuitive, and certain understanding of spiritual realities.

 In essence, تائید in Ismaili thought is the dynamic flow of divine grace and spiritual power that sustains the spiritual hierarchy, illuminates the path to true knowledge, and provides guidance and strength to believers on their spiritual journey. It is fundamental to the Ismaili understanding of the role of the Imam and the nature of spiritual enlightenment.

 

 

 3. Deeper Cosmic Interpretation ( ʿĀlamuʾl-Ibdāʿ ) 

 Adam as the Tenth Intellect: Here, Adam represents the ʿaql al-ʿāshir (العقل العاشر - the Tenth Intellect), also called Ādam Rūḥānī (آدم الروحاني - Spiritual Adam) because of his purely spiritual, non-material nature.

 The Hierarchy of Emanations: The key players are the Sābiq (السابق - the Precedent, the First Emanation/Universal Intellect) and the Tālī (التالي - the Follower, the Second Emanation/Universal Soul). The Tree (in its good aspect) is the rank of the Sābiq (or inbiʿāth al-awwal , الانبعاث الاول - First Emanation), which Adam (the Tenth Intellect) wrongly desired to equal. The Tālī acts as Adam's immediate superior or "Lord" ( rabbuhu , ربه), whose relationship with the Sābiq Adam should have emulated.

 The Fall and Restoration: Adam's sin ( khaṭīʾa , خطيئة) was his ambition, leading to a loss of rank ( inhitāt rutbatihi , انحطاط رتبته) and separation from the divine effluence enjoyed by the other seven Intelligences below him, who actually preceded him in achieving connection to the material world. His repentance came through the Kalimāt (الكلمات - Words, Quran 2:37), identified here as those seven lower Intelligences. By submitting to them ( aṭāʿahā , أطاعها) and using them as intermediaries ( tawassala bihā , وتوسل بها) to the Tālī (who governs his fate and theirs, alladhī malaka amrahu wa-amrahā , الذي ملك أمره وأمرها), the darkness of his delusion ( ẓulām wahmihi wa-ḥayratihi , ظلام وهمه وحيرته) lifted, and he was restored to his original proximity to the Tālī in the spiritual garden ( ḥaẓīrat al-quds , حظيرة القدس - enclosure of holiness).

 Iblis and Descendants: Iblis is explicitly identified with Adam's own flawed thought process – wahm (وهم - defective imagination), ḥayra (حيرة - confusion), inkār (إنكار - denial), and takabbur (تكبر - arrogance). This internal "Iblis" caused his fall and remained fallen ("on earth"), corrupting those souls (his "descendants") who imitated his error. Being "on earth" signifies being in a lower stratum of the spiritual world ( asfal dhālika al-ʿālam , اسفل ذلك العالم). Those descendants who repent can ascend back to the garden like Adam, while the unrepentant join the party of Iblis ( ḥizb Iblīs al-laʿīn , حزب ابليس اللعين) in a state of spiritual separation ("hell", jahannam , جهنم).

 4. Context and Significance 

 Relation to Other Texts: Lewis notes the similarity of these doctrines to the Zahru'l Ma'ānī by the later Yemenite dāʿī Idris and particularly to Druze teachings found in texts like the Risāla Mustaqīma (الرسالة المستقيمة). He suggests this text might be a summary of a fuller tradition that the Druze text represents an earlier version of. Similar ideas about Adam are also found in the Ummu'l-Kitāb (ام الكتاب) used by Ismailis in Central Asia.

 Philosophical Blend: The cosmic interpretation distinctively merges Neoplatonic emanation schemes (likely influenced by figures like Fārābī and Ibn Sīnā, ultimately tracing back through sources like the Theology of Aristotle and Pseudo-Empedocles ) with Gnostic concepts like the Primal or Spiritual Adam ( Ādam Rūḥānī ). Identifying this figure with the Tenth (or Third, accounts vary ) Intellect integrates these different philosophical streams.

 This deeper look shows how Ismaili taʾwīl transforms the seemingly straightforward story of Adam and Eve into a complex allegory about spiritual knowledge, the structure of the cosmos, the path of the soul, and the internal struggle against arrogance and illusion.

 

 

 

 Praise be to God, the Lord of all that exists. May God's peace and blessings be upon Muhammad, the unlettered Prophet, and upon his pure family and descendants.

 Moving on, we previously talked about what it means to have faith (iman) and to submit to God (islam). This is the beginning of understanding the important parts of Islam. We set out to explain these things in this book, matters that God has linked with obeying Him.

 Following this is the topic of loyalty to the messengers and those who are in charge, as God said, "Obey God and obey the Messenger and those in authority among you." So, we have gathered explanations about them, because God has put them together in the duty of obedience, even if their levels and ranks are different. The "Speakers" are the highest in rank, and they are: Adam, Noah, Abraham, Moses, Jesus, and Muhammad, with the seventh being the awaited one of our time.

 After them in importance are the "Foundations," whom God appointed from among them during their lives to convey the inner meanings of faith. The Foundation comes after the Speaker and takes his place, becoming an Imam and establishing proof for himself to stand in his position. This continues until the cycle of that Speaker is complete, and another Speaker comes with a new set of laws. This process goes on as it did in the beginning because the earth is never without someone standing for God with His proof, even for a moment.

 The truthful Imam was asked when the second one would appear after the previous one, and he said, "In the last minute remaining of the previous one's time." This is so that complete excellence would not be found in two at the same time, but only in one after another, as proof of God's oneness, as He said, "If there were in them gods besides Allah, the heavens and the earth would both have been in utter ruin."

 The leader of the time, whether a prophet, a messenger, or an imam, is called a Speaker because he speaks and upholds the outward aspects of religion. His proof is called the Silent One because he is silent about the outward but upholds the inner meanings. He is the reason for the twelve successors whom God mentioned in His book, saying, "And We raised among them twelve leaders." We have already mentioned their proofs in the greater and lesser worlds, and they are like them. That is why we said there are more than we can mention.

 The Imam and the Proof follow each other like night and day, and they are also examples of each other. The Imam is like the day, standing with the outward aspects, and the Proof is like the night, concealing and keeping secret the inner meanings. About this, God said, "And We have made the night and the day two signs."

 We have also mentioned that God's signs in the inner world are His proofs to His creation, and they are the Imams whose obedience He has made obligatory and whom He has commanded us to follow. The title of Imam applies to the Speaker, and the title of Speaker applies to the Imam. God said, "And when Abraham was tried by his Lord with certain commands, which he fulfilled, God said, 'Indeed, I will make you a leader for the people.' Abraham said, 'And of my offspring?' God said, 'My covenant does not include the wrongdoers.'"

 The Imam is called a Speaker because he speaks what the speaking messenger spoke in his time, regarding the outward laws that he was sent with. He is called an Imam because the people follow him, just as the one who leads the prayer is called an Imam for the congregation because they follow him and because he goes before them. So, he is their Imam, meaning he is in front of them, and they are behind him, following him. Similarly, the leader of the prayer is called an Imam for those who follow him. God said, "And We made them leaders inviting to the Fire."

 The title of prophethood is only given to someone who receives divine inspiration directly, without any lower intermediary between him and God. The rank of Imamate is below the rank of prophethood, even though their obedience is equally obligatory, just as it is linked with obedience to God. That is why we must first start by mentioning the origin of Imamate in the lower realm, which is Adam, peace be upon him. Then we will arrange what comes after him, little by little, as much as this level can bear in terms of explanation. So, we say, and with God we seek help: God said, "And when your Lord said to the angels, 'Indeed, I will make upon the earth a successive authority.' They said, 'Will You place upon it one who causes corruption therein and sheds blood, while we declare Your purity and sanctify You?' God said, 'Indeed, I know that which you do not know.'" And His saying, "And when your Lord said to the angels, 'Indeed, I am going to create a human being from clay. So when I have proportioned him and breathed into him of My [created] soul, then fall down to him in prostration.'"   

 

 

 Explanation of Speech and Words Attributed to God

 The first thing that should be mentioned in this discussion from the hidden knowledge of God's close servants is that speech and words attributed to God refer to what makes the listeners understand. This is not like what you see and hear from the speech of speakers and the words of those who talk, from the sounds that come from bodies and cavities, emerge from their places, and are formed by the palate, tongue, and lips. It is also not like the sounds that result from friction between created bodies. God is far above having such things attributed to Him or being compared to anything from His creation.

 In the Arabic language, speech and words can be used without a speaker making a sound or using known words. For example, Arabs say, "The wall leaned and fell," "The man intended and rode," "The world advised its people and addressed them with its lessons," and "Time reproached so-and-so." There are similar expressions in their speech. So, God addressed them in a way they understood.

 A person can see in their dream what they have not seen while awake and hear words without a speaker or a sound without someone making it. In God's power, His command is conveyed from Him to the one commanded as He wills, without any partner. God, the most truthful speaker, has said, "Our word to a thing when We intend it is only that We say to it, 'Be,' and it is." So, His saying "Be, and it is" is not like how created beings say it. The word "Be" consists of two letters that make up speech, one moving and the other silent. This is an example of the two fundamental principles. There are many examples.

 What is in God's secret knowledge with His close servants, the angels whom God mentioned, is that what He said to them about Adam was not addressed to all the angels. But when God wanted to create Adam, the second creation, which is the creation of knowledge, as He said, "Then We produced him as another creation," and "He creates you in the wombs of your mothers, creation after creation," and to appoint him as a leader, He chose twelve angels to make him their chief, from whom they would receive knowledge and convey it to the people of the earth.

 When He informed them that He would create him from clay, and clay in the inner sense is dense, outward, argumentative knowledge, and God had created the angels in the creation of subtle, spiritual, inner knowledge, it was previously known to them that subtle knowledge does not connect with matter, and dense knowledge only comes through teaching. Also, spiritual, subtle knowledge is not related to actions; it is knowledge without action or obligation, like outward knowledge, as the Commander of the Believers said when mentioning the Quran, "Its outward is obligatory knowledge, and its inward is hidden knowledge."

 Therefore, the angels were not tested with actions. It was previously known to them that anyone who is obligated to act will inevitably fall short and make mistakes. They said, "Will You place upon it one who causes corruption therein and sheds blood, while we declare Your purity and sanctify You?" God meant that He would grant Adam the same support He granted them and would help him if he fell short or made a mistake, rescuing him from destruction.

 Among them was Iblis (Satan), who thought himself better than Adam and more deserving of the rank. He disdained being subordinate to him, a means for him, or receiving knowledge from him. He was arrogant towards the command of his Lord. God wanted to show the angels their limitations, so He created Adam, as He said, from clay, meaning He began him with outward knowledge, just as He begins in the way He established for His servants. He remained in that state for a long time, knowing only what he was taught. This is what is mentioned in the tradition that when God created a form from clay, it remained a lifeless body for a long time before He breathed into it the spirit, meaning the inner, spiritual knowledge.

 Iblis used to come to him and exchange knowledge with him, but he found nothing with him except the outward knowledge. So, Iblis was pleased with that and reported that Adam was hollow, meaning empty of inner knowledge and without it. This was before Iblis and the angels appointed for this purpose were commanded to obey Adam, because God only said to them, "So when I have proportioned him and breathed into him of My [created] soul, then fall down to him in prostration," meaning submit to him in obedience when I provide him with spiritual knowledge.

 Iblis, from among them, kept an eye on Adam because of the enmity he harbored for him and the envy and arrogance he felt towards him. He saw himself as better than Adam and was pleased to see his lack of inner knowledge, feeling resentment and pride. When God wanted to honor Adam, He supplied him with that knowledge and taught it to him, as He said, "And He taught Adam the names - all of them." This means the names of everything that humanity needs to know, because everything has a name. When He taught him that, He presented him to the angels who were qualified to connect with him and began by asking them about what He had taught Adam. They did not have the knowledge to answer. At that point, they said what God recounted about them, "Exalted are You; we have no knowledge except what You have taught us. Indeed, it is You who is the Knowing, the Wise." God said, "O Adam, inform them of their names." He said, "Did I not tell you that I know the unseen [aspects] of the heavens and the earth? And I know what you reveal and what you have concealed."   

 

 

 

 

 

 

 The truthful Imam, peace be upon him, said: I went on pilgrimage with my father one year. He circumambulated the Kaaba one night, and I circumambulated with him. Then he went to the Stone, prayed, and I prayed with him until I got tired and sat down. He was still standing and praying when a man approached and sat beside him while he was praying. I looked at a man of perfect build, large bones, with reddish-brown skin and hair, wearing two thick white garments. When my father saw him sitting beside him, he shortened his prayer, then gave the salutation and turned to him, saying, "Do you have any need?" He said, "I have come to ask you about the beginning of the creation of this House."

 My father said to him, "Yes, when God created Adam and mentioned his story and what happened with the angels and what they said to Him, and He said to them, 'Indeed, I know what you reveal and what you have concealed.'" He said, "As for what they revealed, it was their saying, 'Will You place upon it one who causes corruption therein and sheds blood, while we declare Your purity and sanctify You?' But what they concealed was that they said to themselves, 'We did not think that God would create a creation better than us and command us to prostrate to it.' When He said that to them, they knew they had fallen into disobedience, so they took refuge in the Throne and circumambulated it for a week, seeking their Lord's pleasure. He was pleased with them and said to them, 'Descend to the earth and build a house there for whoever sins among the children of Adam to take refuge in, and circumambulate it as you took refuge in the Throne and circumambulated it. Then I will be pleased with them as I was pleased with you.' So, they built this House, then God raised it up during the Flood, and it is in the fourth heaven, where the angels perform pilgrimage until the Day of Resurrection. Abraham, the Friend [of God], built this House on its foundation." He then mentioned the rest of the narration, which is long, and we have shortened it to the necessary part.

 Then God commanded the angels to prostrate to Adam, meaning He commanded them to obey him, connect with him, and accept from him, as God said, "Do you not see that to Allah prostrates whoever is in the heavens and whoever is on the earth, and the sun, the moon, the stars, the mountains, the trees, the moving creatures and many of the people? But there are many for whom punishment is justified." So, they prostrated as He commanded them, "except for Iblis; he refused and was arrogant and became of the disbelievers." He was only named Iblis after this, when he despaired. The scholars of language say that Iblis is derived from the root "ablase," meaning to despair. It is said that a man "ablase" when he despairs. Similarly, Iblis, when he despaired of the rank he held, became "ablase." It is also said that a man "ablase" when he is cut off and has no argument, or when he is sad. "Balasa" means to be humble. All these conditions came together in Iblis. God, the most truthful speaker, said, "And the Day the Hour appears the criminals will be in despair." And God said, "What prevented you from prostrating when I commanded you?" He said, "I am better than him. You created me from fire and created him from clay." He meant that He created him with spiritual knowledge and bright, supportive knowledge that the physical body cannot sustain, and He created Adam with dense, thick, logical, natural, argumentative knowledge. So, how could he prostrate to someone he considered better than himself? He revealed what he had kept secret and openly disobeyed. God became angry with him and cursed him, meaning He banished him, saying, "Get out of Paradise, for indeed, you are driven away. And indeed, upon you is My curse until the Day of Recompense." So, He expelled him from the realm of support, cut it off from him, and banished him from it. Curse in the language means banishment.   

 

 God created Eve from Adam, as He said, "And created from it its mate." This refers to the creation of support, not the physical creation. God commanded Adam, so he undertook her support, teaching, and guidance, and He paired her with him, making her his wife. She is his proof, a replacement for Iblis, not as the common people believe, that God caused Adam to sleep, then took one of his ribs and created Eve from it. They only heard a part of the explanation and did not understand or comprehend it. They heard that God replaced a rib of Adam and created Eve from that rib, but that is not the case. Rather, God compensated Adam with Eve instead of Iblis, who was qualified to be Adam's proof, as we said, and made him one of the twelve chiefs, because their counterparts in the outward creation of humans are the ribs of the body. A human has twelve ribs on each side. The right side represents inner knowledge, and the left side represents outward knowledge. The chiefs with inner knowledge are twelve, and similarly, their counterparts for outward knowledge are also twelve.

 Then God settled Adam and his wife in Paradise, which in the inner sense is the realm of the messengers' support, from them down to the chiefs. Our explanation of the inner meaning in this and other matters does not negate the outward meaning, not at all, because it is a duality. God says, "And of all things We have created [in] pairs; perhaps you will remember." Paradise is one of the greatest things God created, and He has made it good news for the believers, saying, "On the Day you see the believing men and believing women, their light proceeding before them and on their right, [it will be said], 'Your good tidings today are [of] gardens beneath which rivers flow, wherein you will abide eternally.' That is the great success." And He said, "But for him who fears the standing before his Lord are two gardens." Then He said, "And give good tidings to those who believe and do righteous deeds that they will have gardens beneath which rivers flow. Whenever they are provided with fruit therefrom, they will say, 'This is what we were provided with before.' And they will be given [therein] similar [kinds]." So, God made Paradise good news for the believers and said in another place, "For them are good tidings in the worldly life and in the Hereafter." He informed us that they have Paradise in both this life and the next, and His saying, "Whenever they are provided with fruit therefrom, they will say, 'This is what we were provided with before,'" confirms that.   

 

 

 So, let the listener not imagine that when we mention the inner meaning of Paradise, we deny that there is an eternal Paradise and a place of bliss. And when we mention the inner meaning of Hellfire, we do not deny that there is a fire and a place of punishment. Or whenever we mention the inner interpretation of something, we invalidate its outward meaning. We seek refuge in God from that, because no outward form can stand without an inner meaning, just as we do not see in this world a soul residing in human beings except within a body, and there is no inner meaning without an outward form. And without the outward form, the inner meaning cannot be named. So, understand this well, O believers, with complete and perfect understanding, and let it penetrate your hearts, for most of those who perished from the people of this knowledge perished from this aspect, and because of it, Adam fell into sin. We will mention that in its time.

 So, believe in the outward and inward meanings of what God has revealed, and have faith in it and act upon it. God said, "And leave what is outwardly sinful and inwardly sinful." And He said, "And He has completed and perfected His favors upon you, [both] outwardly and inwardly." So, what is outwardly lawful is lawful, and its inward meaning is also lawful. What is outwardly forbidden is forbidden, and its inward meaning is also forbidden. Everything, as we mentioned, has an outward and an inward meaning. This is the outward and the inward.

 As for the parables that God has given to His servants, saying, "And these examples We present to the people, but none will understand them except those of knowledge," and "Indeed, Allah is not ashamed to present an example - that of a mosquito or what is smaller than it. And as for those who believe, they know that it is the truth from their Lord. But as for those who disbelieve, they say, 'What did Allah intend by this example?' He misleads many thereby and guides many thereby. And He misleads not except the defiantly disobedient, who break the covenant of Allah after its confirmation," and His saying, "And We have certainly presented for the people in this Quran from every [kind of] example," it is not the case with the outward and inward meanings that the example and what it represents are fixed and established. Rather, what is fixed and established is the represented, which is like the example, similar to His saying, "Guide us to the straight path - The path of those upon whom You have bestowed favor, not of those who have evoked [Your] anger or of those who are astray." The straight path in the language means the way. So, the Imam here is like the way, because whoever stays on the way will not go astray, and similarly, whoever stays with the Imam will not go astray. The intended meaning of the way here is the Imam, not the path walked upon on the earth.   

 Returning to the mention of Adam, peace be upon him, and to the inner meaning of Paradise, which is the support of true, pure, spiritual, subtle knowledge, because the bliss in it surpasses all bliss, and the pleasure in it surpasses all pleasure. Because of this, whoever understands it becomes detached from all worldly attachments, and they become insignificant and small in his eyes. Everything in this world becomes easy for him due to his connection with that knowledge and his union with it. He finds it to be the complete bliss and the honor after which there is no humiliation. From this is the saying of the Commander of the Believers, Ali, peace be upon him, to Kumayl ibn Ziyad, in which he said, "Knowledge is a ruler, and wealth is ruled over. Wealth diminishes with spending, while knowledge increases with giving. The treasurers of wealth have died, but the scholars remain as long as time remains. Their persons are lost, but their آثار ( آثار - traces, legacies) exist in the world." It was a long saying, from which we have mentioned some of what is needed.

 And God said, "O Adam, dwell, you and your wife, in Paradise and eat from it freely from wherever you will. But do not approach this tree, lest you be among the wrongdoers." And He warned him against Iblis, saying, "Indeed, this is an enemy to you and to your wife. So let him not remove you from Paradise and cause you to suffer." And He made a covenant with him about that, saying, "And We had already taken a covenant from Adam before, but he forgot; and We found not in him determination." So, God allowed them the realms of the messengers with their conditions and obligations that He had imposed upon them, including making lawful what He made lawful, forbidding what He forbade, and preventing what He prohibited and warned against. He made the tree that He forbade him to approach, prevented him from it, and made it unlawful for him, an example of the realm of the awaited Imam of our time, who is the master of the Resurrection. In his realm, obligations are lifted if they were not acted upon before, as God said, "There will not benefit a soul its faith if it had not believed before or earned through its faith some good. And no ransom will be accepted from it, nor will they be helped." At that time, the inner meaning will be revealed, and the outward form will fall away. The inner meaning will become apparent, contrary to what it was in the realms of the messengers before that, because in their realms, it is entrusted to their proofs, hidden with them, and passed from one to another. It is a miracle for them and a guiding light until it reaches its owner, meaning the master of the Resurrection, who will reveal it and make it apparent. That was forbidden and prohibited before him. So, God initially forbade Adam from that so that God's way, which has no change, would proceed upon him.

 Iblis asked for a respite until the Day of the Appointed Time, and by the Day, he meant the master of the Resurrection. He said, "If You delay me until the Day of Resurrection, I will surely mislead his descendants, except for a few." God said, "Go, and whoever of them follows you - indeed, Hell will be your recompense, an ample recompense. And incite [to injustice] whomever you can among them with your voice, and assault them with your horses and foot soldiers, and share with them in [their] wealth and children." By wealth, He meant knowledge, if he introduces doubt and falsehood into it for them. By children, He meant those born into faith, because the followers of the inner call are like his children. God said, "The religion of your father, Abraham. He has named you 'Muslims' before." The Speaker is like a father to the believers, and the Proof is like a mother, because she carries them, nurses them with knowledge, and raises them. That is why the Messenger of God, peace and blessings be upon him, said, "I and you, O Ali, are the fathers of this nation." So, by His saying, "and share with them in their children," God meant the believers who truly became their children. Whoever you overcome with your falsehood among them, you have snatched him from their birth, which is the birth of faith, to your birth, which is the birth of disbelief. So, share with them in that and promise them with your deception and falsehood. God made that a trial for them.

 Then God informed us about His sincere servants that Iblis will not prevail over them and that He will completely return them to Himself, and they will be of His party and His children. He said, "Indeed, My servants - no authority will you have over them." And He made a party for Himself from among them and said, describing the party of Satan, "Have you not seen those who take as allies a people with whom Allah has become angry? They are neither of you nor of them, and they swear to falsehood while they know. Allah has prepared for them a severe punishment. Indeed, evil is what they have been doing. They have taken their oaths as a cover, so they have averted [people] from the way of Allah. For them will be a humiliating punishment. Their wealth will not avail them, nor will their children, against Allah at all. Those are the companions of the Fire; they will abide therein eternally. On the Day Allah will resurrect them all, they will swear to Him as they swear to you and think that they are [standing] on something [good]. Unquestionably, it is they who are the liars. Satan has overcome them and made them forget the remembrance of Allah. Those are the party of Satan. Unquestionably, the party of Satan - they will be the losers."   

 

 

 Then He described His party, saying, "You will not find a people who believe in Allah and the Last Day having affection for those who oppose Allah and His Messenger, even if they were their fathers or their sons or their brothers or their relatives. Those - Allah has decreed within their hearts faith and supported them with spirit from Him. And We will admit them to gardens beneath which rivers flow, wherein they will abide eternally. Allah is pleased with them, and they are pleased with Him. Those are the party of Allah. Unquestionably, the party of Allah - they are the successful." So, God clarified that His party are those who are devoted to Him, who take His close servants as allies, and that the party of Satan are those whom Satan has overcome, snatched for himself, and shared with God's close servants. So, they took His enemies and their enemies as allies, and they disavowed His allegiance and their allegiance, and they took Satan and his allies as allies.   

 

 

 As for whoever Satan has humiliated and then regrets his humiliation, repents from his stumbling, and turns to his Lord, he is not of Satan or his party. God said, "And whoever does a wrong or wrongs himself but then seeks forgiveness of Allah will find Allah Forgiving and Merciful." The truthful Imam, peace be upon him, said, "You will not find a close servant whose feet both slip, but when one foot slips, he relies on the other." So, the accursed Iblis humiliated Adam and Eve, peace be upon them, by saying to them what God recounted about him in His book, "Your Lord did not forbid you this tree except that you should become angels or become of the immortals." And he swore to them, "Indeed, I am to you from among the sincere advisors." He made the limit of permissibility seem appealing to them in what God had warned them against, and he said to them that the limit of acting upon obligations is the limit of toil and hardship, and if they left it, they would become like the subtle, spiritual angels and be free from the dense, physical world, but they would be two immortal angels in whatever they desired. That is His saying, "Then Satan whispered to them to make apparent to them that which was concealed from them of their private parts. And he said, 'Your Lord did not forbid you this tree except that you should become angels or become of the immortals.' And he swore to them, 'Indeed, I am to you from among the sincere advisors.' So he made them fall through deception. And when they tasted of the tree, their private parts became apparent to them, and they began to fasten together over themselves from the leaves of Paradise. And their Lord called to them, 'Did I not forbid you that tree and tell you that Satan is to you a clear enemy?'"   

 

 He shouted, meaning he exceeded the limits of the Speakers that were allowed to them to the limit of the Resurrection that God had set for it. They reached out to what was not theirs, took from it, and ate from the tree, so they fell into sin and disobedience. Support was cut off from them, which is the garment of righteousness that God mentioned in His book, saying, "But the garment of righteousness - that is best of the signs of Allah." And they were stripped of it, and their private parts became apparent to them, meaning what was unseemly in them from the density of the natural form became visible when they were stripped of the garment of righteousness, meaning the word that concealed every darkness within it with its light, because support does not accompany sins, and reaching out to what is not allowed causes a feeling like its severing.

 When support was cut off from them, they began, as God said, "to fasten together over themselves from the leaves of Paradise," meaning they began to cling to the light of wisdom through remembrance to cover the shame of what had become apparent from them and what had been uncovered of their state with the removal of support and from ignorance of the limits. Many have perished who clung to this matter and went to permissibility without limits. And their Lord called to them, as God said, "Did I not forbid you that tree and tell you that Satan is to you a clear enemy?" They said, "Our Lord, we have wronged ourselves, and if You do not forgive us and have mercy upon us, we will surely be among the losers." A pen rusts on the wolf, just as Iblis rusted on it. Rather, they repented to God from it, turned to Him, and asked for His mercy and forgiveness. "Then Adam received from his Lord [some] words, and He accepted his repentance. Indeed, it is He who is the Accepting of repentance, the Merciful," meaning he drew near to Him and received His acceptance of repentance from the direction of the higher realms. So, He accepted his repentance, as God said. Iblis had no authority over him to drag him to himself and overcome him until he became of his party. Rather, he was of the party of God through his repentance to Him, His forgiveness for him, and his receiving His words.   

 

 Similarly, whoever among his descendants commits evil and then repents from it and comes to Him through His close servants, he will be forgiven and will be of His party. But whoever persists and continues in his sin and does not turn to Him and does not come to Him through His close servants, he will be of the party and soldiers of Iblis. God said, "And if, when they wronged themselves, they had come to you, [O Muhammad], and asked forgiveness of Allah, and the Messenger had asked forgiveness for them, they would have found Allah Accepting of repentance and Merciful." So, the repentance of those who repent is not accepted except by receiving His words, and they are not forgiven except after their asking for forgiveness and the words asking for forgiveness for them.   

 

 This is the explanation of the parable of the tree that God forbade Adam, peace be upon him, from, and what happened from his taking from it, the uncovering of his private parts, and the beginning of his shame. This is an inner interpretation, and it is in the detailed explanation in this book, not as the common people have interpreted it and taken it literally, that the tree was a wheat tree. Others said it was a fig tree, and others said it was a grape tree, and that their clothing was made of fingernails, which fell away from them, and repentance reached it, and some of it remained on the tips of their fingers and remained in their children. Most animals have claws and similar things, like the hooves of animals, the cloven hooves of cattle and sheep, and the talons of predators and birds, but they are not from the offspring of Adam, nor did what happened to him happen to them, so it could not have remained on their tips. Rather, it is wisdom in their composition that the Creator arranged and determined. If the clothing were made of fingernails, its meaning in the interpretation would be something that minds cannot accept and is not valid in understanding.

 Similarly, what they claim, that Adam and Eve's private parts were uncovered, so they sewed for themselves coverings from fig leaves, that also does not hold true, as it did not happen. God is far exalted and greater than to do that to a prophet He has chosen and honored. If one of those who interpret it that way looked at a person who was angry with his servant and took off his clothes, leaving him naked and exposed, he would consider that action foolish. Yet they attribute that to their Lord! God is far above that. Most of their sayings and this interpretation they took from those who converted from their ancestors among the Jews, such as Ka'b al-Ahbar, Abdullah ibn Salam, and others, because they claim that it is found in their Torah that Adam and Eve remained naked in Paradise and felt the presence of God walking among its trees, so they hid from Him among its trees. He called to them, "Where are you?" They said, "We felt you walking among the trees of Paradise, and we were naked, so we were ashamed of you and hid." He said to them, "Have you eaten from the tree?" Adam said, "The woman gave me to eat," and the woman said, "The serpent commanded me," and the serpent said, "Iblis entered my belly." God is far above this interpretation, that the regions could contain it or that eyes could see it.

 In the Torah and the Gospel, there are also parables, allusions, symbols, and outward meanings that need interpretation, which is the hidden knowledge, just like in the Quran. God said, "Say, 'O People of the Scripture, you are [standing] on nothing until you uphold [the law of] the Torah, the Gospel, and what has been revealed to you from your Lord.'" From this, it is known that they do not uphold that except through interpretation. That is why it was said in interpretation that the Torah is an example of the outward, and the Gospel is an example of the inward. So, God commanded upholding both of them in the way we mentioned earlier, with the knowledge that God bestowed upon Adam and transmitted through the Speakers, Foundations, Imams, and their successors from their descendants, peace and blessings be upon them all.   

 

 As for what preceded from God's saying, "And their Lord called to them," His calling to them is a connection from Him to them, not that it was a call like the call heard from those who make sounds. Similarly, what we mention about His saying and His words, blessed be His name, and we have already given a long explanation about that before this. Rather, the servants were addressed with that and similar things that are inconsistent with God and do not befit Him, but what is commonly known among the servants is that sound only comes from someone making a sound, and from a cavity and palate, and speech only comes through lips and tongues, and similarly what comes from the collision of bodies. God is far exalted above that.

 When God accepted Adam's repentance, He did not lower him from the rank of succession and Imamate that He had established for them, nor did He completely deprive him of His essence, words, and wisdom. But He caused him and Eve to descend from the realms of interpretation without any veil, saying, "Descend, [some of you] being enemies to others. And for you on the earth is a place of settlement and enjoyment for a time." And He said, "Therein you will live, and therein you will die, and from it you will be brought forth." And He said, "O children of Adam, We have bestowed upon you clothing to conceal your private parts and as adornment. But the clothing of righteousness - that is best. That is from the signs of Allah that they might remember."   

 

 So, God caused Adam to descend from the realm of interpretation without a veil, but He did not deprive him of the essence. So, what he was connected to from the essence began to descend upon him from the higher spiritual realms and was transmitted from him to the lower realms. That is from the signs of God, as He mentioned. And that continued in the Speakers from his descendants. The Messenger of God, peace and blessings be upon him, said, "Indeed, I receive revelation from Gabriel, and Gabriel receives it from Michael, and Michael receives it from Israfil, and Israfil receives it from the Tablet, and the Tablet receives it from the Pen." So, support became connected to the Speakers from five higher realms, and it is transmitted from them to the responders through five lower realms. The higher realms are those we mentioned, and the lower realms are the Yesterday, the Imams, the Proofs, the Chiefs of the Islands, and the Wings, who are the callers. The sixth of the two realms is the Receiver.

 Iblis became an enemy to Adam and his opposite. Everyone who is loyal to God, whether Speaker or Silent One, Imam or Proof, and whoever is loyal to them and affiliated with them, is of the party of Adam, which is the party of God. Everyone who opposes and fights God's close servants and distances himself from their command or claims their rank is of the party of Iblis, whom Satan has overcome and made them forget the remembrance of God. Forgetting here means delaying, as in he delayed them from the remembrance of God. Remembrance of God here in the inner sense is His close servants, by whom He is remembered, and they are the ones who brought His remembrance.

 Similarly, what happened between Adam and Eve happened in their descendants. Even if their descendants are many, they do not exceed their two genders, male and female, just as Adam and Eve were. And similarly, in all of them, obedience and disobedience occurred as it did in Adam and Eve. Adam's beginning in his creation and what happened to him is similar to what happens to his descendants in the beginning of their lives and their being in this world. God's settling Adam and Eve in Paradise after what happened to them is similar to God settling those among their descendants who believe and do good deeds and are of their party. The repentance of whoever repents among them is like their repentance, and the destruction of whoever is destroyed among them by disobedience and goes to God's punishment due to his disbelief and committing what He forbade, is due to Iblis overcoming them and their being of his party and their separation from Adam and leaving his lineage, so they entered the place of those they became from and separated from him, meaning from Adam, due to their separation from him. God said, "And whoever of you allies himself with them - indeed, he is [one] of them." Whoever allies himself with Iblis is of him, and they are his children through the call to him and his people through connection with him. They separated from Adam, as God said about Noah's son when he disobeyed him, "Indeed, he is not of your family." God named the party of Satan and called them by his name, and said, "Indeed, Satan is to you a clear enemy." And He said, "Indeed, My servants - no authority will you have over them," meaning the followers of Adam among his descendants, "except those who follow you of the deviators. And indeed, Hell is their appointed place - all of them. It has seven gates; for each gate is of them a portion determined." By the gates, He means the messengers, who are the pure outward gates, because each of them has a known law, and a group who reject it will have their love directed towards the Fire. From this is the saying of the Messenger of God, peace and blessings be upon him, to Ali, "And you are the keeper of the Fire." That is why he will say to it on the Day of Resurrection, "This is for you, so take it, and this is for me, so leave it."

 The meaning of this is that Iblis's opposition and enmity towards Adam are like the opposition and enmity of the enemies of the messengers towards them. God said, "And thus We have made for every prophet an enemy among the criminals." So, the sinner from the children of Adam who persists in his sin, does not repent from it, and does not give it up, his example is like the example of Iblis in his persistence. He has left the party of Adam and entered his party, entered his place, and will go to God's punishment with him, meaning with Iblis. But the one who repents, gives up what he has committed, and is obedient, he is from the children of Adam and his party, which is the party of God. God said, "And those who, when they commit an immorality or wrong themselves [by transgression], remember Allah and seek forgiveness for their sins - and who can forgive sins except Allah? - and [who] do not persist in what they have done while they know. Those - their reward is forgiveness from their Lord and gardens beneath which rivers flow, wherein they will abide eternally; and excellent is the reward of the [righteous] workers."   

 

 So, God supported Adam with the testament, settled him on the earth, and made him a successor over those on it. His descendants multiplied on it, and he was God's proof to them and the speaker of the message to them. He bequeathed to his son Abel, so his brother Cain envied him and killed him, as God described. That was in Adam's lifetime, so that the testament would go to him, in what he imagined and thought, and he persisted in his disobedience and remained in his sin, and he was of the party of Iblis. God bestowed upon Adam from his descendants Seth, and he named him "Gift of God" and entrusted him with knowledge and confided in him and commanded him to conceal it, as a precaution against Cain killing him as he killed Abel. Thus, the tradition continued in the secrets of the covenant and its preservation. God directed Adam to what He had prepared for him of reward in the abode of permanence. His successor Seth took charge of the Imamate after him, and matters continued in his descendants according to what we mentioned earlier, of a successor after a successor and an Imam after an Imam. Among those whom God mentioned in His book is Idris, peace be upon him, and he is from the cycle of Adam, peace be upon him. Then the matter ended with Noah, peace be upon him. God sent him as a messenger speaking a law different from the law of Adam. He said, "For every nation We have appointed a law and a method." The cycle of Adam ended with the coming of Noah, peace be upon them. Each of them did not undertake the message except after adhering to the law of the one before him, and the covenant of that law had been taken from him, and he adhered to it.
